    contents:
      - content: GET /api/v1/accesslog/online-users/ — live admin sessions.
        heading: api_v1_accesslog_online-users_list
      - content: >-
          Admin action: turn OFF two-factor for another user — e.g. when they
          have

          lost their device. Removes their TOTP and backup devices.


          If the target still has `require_2fa` set they will be sent back
          through

          setup on next sign-in — this is a reset, not an exemption. To exempt
          them, an

          admin also unticks "Require two-factor authentication".
        heading: api_v1_account_2fa_admin-disable_create
      - content: Hand the SPA a CSRF token to send on subsequent writes.
        heading: api_v1_account_csrf_list
      - content: >-
          Current user + setup state — the SPA reads this on load/refresh to
          decide

          where to send the user (dashboard vs /setup). A 403 tells the SPA to
          show the

          login page.


          A session that has passed the password step but NOT the 2FA token step
          is

          only half-authenticated: the login view creates it (so `verify` can
          complete

          it) but it must not count as signed-in for the app. django-otp exposes
          this

          as `user.is_verified()` — a user with a device who has not verified
          fails it.

          Treat that as "not logged in" so the SPA keeps showing the token step
          rather

          than falling through to the dashboard.
        heading: api_v1_account_me_list
      - content: >-
          Uses ALLAUTH's ResetPasswordForm, not Django's — this project's
          reset-confirm

          flow is allauth's (`account_reset_password_from_key`), so Django's
          form crashed

          with NoReverseMatch trying to reverse `password_reset_confirm`.
          Allauth builds

          the correct link + email its own confirm view expects.


          Always answers 200 — never reveal whether an address is registered (an

          enumeration leak). A send failure is logged, not surfaced, for the
          same

          reason.
        heading: send-a-password-reset-email-for-the-given-address
      - content: >-
          Set a new password from a valid reset link. Applies the configured

          password validators (via allauth's ResetPasswordKeyForm) so weak
          passwords

          are rejected with a readable message.
        heading: api_v1_account_reset_confirm_create
      - content: >-
          Check that a reset link is still valid, so the React page can show the

          set-password form (or an "expired link" message) before the user
          types.
        heading: api_v1_account_reset_verify_create
      - content: >-
          Lists BackupMetadata rather than Template(kind='backup'): the Template
          row

          carries only a generated name, while everything that identifies a
          backup to

          a person — which device, why it was taken, when — lives on the
          metadata.


          Read-only. Backups are written by the snapshot task; the one mutation
          that

          makes sense (restore) pushes server-side through the existing
          PushedConfig

          flow and is not part of this list.
        heading: config-backups--the-react-equivalent-of-the-admins-backup-templates-tab
      - content: >-
          Delete removes the metadata AND the paired Template. The two are one
          object

          to a user, and dropping only the metadata would leave an orphaned

          "Backup — device — timestamp" row in the ordinary template list.
        heading: fetch-or-delete-one-backup
      - content: >-
          Delete removes the metadata AND the paired Template. The two are one
          object

          to a user, and dropping only the metadata would leave an orphaned

          "Backup — device — timestamp" row in the ordinary template list.
        heading: fetch-or-delete-one-backup-1
      - content: >-
          ``POST`` rather than ``GET`` because the encrypted format takes a

          passphrase, and a passphrase in a query string ends up in the access
          log,

          the browser history and any proxy in between.


          Two formats, both of the same content — the verbatim UCI export the
          backup

          was taken from:

            * ``plain``     — the text as stored, readable in any editor.
            * ``encrypted`` — the same text inside an ``openssl enc`` envelope keyed
              by the caller's passphrase (see :mod:`config_compare.crypto`).

          Authorisation is stricter than the list endpoint on purpose: the list

          withholds ``raw_uci_text`` precisely because it carries PSKs, WiFi
          keys and

          user passwords, so downloading it requires being a manager of the
          owning

          organization, not merely being logged in.
        heading: hand-one-stored-backup-to-the-operator-as-a-file
      - content: GET/PUT/DELETE a saved config.
        heading: api_v1_config-compare_saved_read
      - content: GET/PUT/DELETE a saved config.
        heading: api_v1_config-compare_saved_partial_update
      - content: GET/PUT/DELETE a saved config.
        heading: api_v1_config-compare_saved_delete
      - content: GET/PUT/DELETE a saved config.
        heading: api_v1_config-compare_saved_update
      - content: Current rollout phase and recent sync timeline for one device.
        heading: api_v1_config-sync_device_read
      - content: Rollout of one template across every device it is applied to.
        heading: api_v1_config-sync_template_read
      - content: Renews the CA.
        heading: api_v1_controller_ca_renew_create
      - content: Renews the Certificate.
        heading: api_v1_controller_cert_renew_create
      - content: Revokes the Certificate.
        heading: api_v1_controller_cert_revoke_create
      - content: >-
          The REST equivalent of the admin page's second tab. Note what this is
          NOT:

          applying a backup is not attaching a template. A backup holds the
          verbatim

          UCI text pulled off a device, so it cannot be attached — it is pushed
          over

          SSH, through the same PushedConfig + push_device_config path the
          Config

          Compare screen uses. That is why the response says queued and points
          at

          Pushed Config for progress.


          Destructive by nature: it overwrites the running configuration of
          every

          selected device with a snapshot taken from (usually) a different one.
          The

          org and manager checks below are the same as bulk apply-template's;
          the

          caller is expected to confirm intent before getting here.
        heading: push-one-backups-stored-configuration-to-many-devices
      - content: >-
          The REST equivalent of the admin's bulk-apply-template page, which the

          React device list previously had no way to reach. Same rules as that
          view,

          restated here rather than imported because it is a TemplateResponse
          flow

          tangled up with forms and redirects:

            * every selected device must be in ONE organization — templates are
              org-scoped, so a mixed selection has no single valid template list
            * the caller must manage that organization (superusers excepted)
            * a device with no Config gets one, otherwise there is nothing to
              attach templates to
            * mode 'add' unions with what is already applied, 'replace' sets

          Wrapped in a transaction: a partial apply across a fleet is worse than
          a

          clean failure, because you cannot tell by looking which half took.
        heading: apply-templates-to-many-devices-at-once
      - content: >-
          The REST equivalent of config_compare's "Create config backup
          (snapshot

          now)" admin action, and it calls the very same Celery task, so the two

          routes cannot drift.


          Queues rather than snapshots: the task SSHes to the device. It also
          skips

          writing a duplicate when nothing changed since the last backup, which
          is

          why the response says "queued" and not "created".
        heading: queue-an-on-demand-config-snapshot-for-each-selected-device
      - content: |-
          Mirrors the admin's "Recover deleted" list, scoped to the user's
          organizations (superusers see all). Read-only.
        heading: devices-that-were-deleted-but-are-still-recoverable-from-django-reversion
      - content: Recover one deleted device (and the related objects in its revision).
        heading: api_v1_controller_device_deleted_recover_create
      - content: Execute a command on a device
        heading: execute-device-command
      - content: >-
          The JSON sibling of ``device/<pk>/configuration/``, which returns the
          same

          thing as a .tar.gz for the agent to download.


          Distinct from ``Config.config`` on the device detail endpoint: that
          field

          is only the device's *own* overrides. A device configured entirely
          from

          templates — the normal case — has ``{}`` there while the rendered
          output is

          full, so a UI reading the raw field shows nothing precisely when the
          most

          has been configured.
        heading: the-devices-configuration-as-the-backend-renders-it-in-json
      - content: Shows only locations which are assigned to devices.
        heading: api_v1_controller_location_geojson_list
      - content: Push this user immediately to all devices in their org.
        heading: api_v1_cpeusers_local-user_deploy
      - content: >-
          Runs server-side PRE-FLIGHT safety checks; refuses (4xx, no Celery
          task

          enqueued) when the cluster is not healthy enough. Every accepted
          action is

          audit-logged (DCDREvent) and executed by `run_dcdr_action` via SCOPED
          sudo —

          the web/celery process gets NO blanket sudo.
        heading: post-apiv1dcdraction--superuser-only-guided-repmgr-switchover
      - content: >-
          Superuser-only. Releases the single in-flight lock once the action is
          done so

          a new action can start.
        heading: get-apiv1dcdractionprogress--poll-the-in-flight-actions-progress
      - content: |-
          POST /api/v1/dcdr/event/
          Record a failover/fallback event from scripts.
          Requires token authentication + admin privileges.
        heading: api_v1_dcdr_event_create
      - content: Returns {count, next, previous, results}.
        heading: get-apiv1dcdrevents--paginated-dcdr-event-history-page_size-25
      - content: >-
          GET /api/v1/dcdr/peer-status/

          Called by the OTHER server to get this server's status.

          Requires token auth + admin (prevents device tokens from querying
          infra topology).
        heading: api_v1_dcdr_peer-status_list
      - content: >-
          GET /api/v1/dcdr/status/

          Returns live DC/DR infrastructure status. Polled every 10s by the
          Fabric UI.
        heading: api_v1_dcdr_status_list

        heading: api_v1_firmware-audit_read
      - content: >-
          ``GET /api/v1/firmware-pre-upgrade-backup/?device=<uuid>``


          The device firmware page reads this alongside the upgrade-operation
          list

          and joins on ``upgrade_operation`` — one row per flash, carrying
          whether a

          backup was taken, of what, and whether anyone has downloaded it.


          Read-only and org-scoped the same way as the audit log: the rows
          describe

          devices, so a user sees the records of devices in their organizations.
        heading: pre-upgrade-backup-records-newest-first

          Returns a list of objects (DeviceFirmware and Device)
          which would be upgraded if POST is used.
        heading: api_v1_firmware-upgrader_build_upgrade_list
      - content: Upgrades all the devices related to the specified build ID.
        heading: api_v1_firmware-upgrader_build_upgrade_create
      - content: |-
          **API Endpoint used by FreeRADIUS server.**
          Returns a list of accounting objects
        heading: api_v1_freeradius_accounting_list
      - content: |-
          **API Endpoint used by FreeRADIUS server.**
          Add or update accounting information (start, interim-update, stop);
          does not return any JSON response so that freeradius will avoid
          processing the response without generating warnings
        heading: api_v1_freeradius_accounting_create
      - content: >-
          **API Endpoint used by FreeRADIUS server.**

          It's triggered when a user submits the form to login into the captive
          portal.

          The captive portal has to be configured to send the password to
          freeradius

          in clear text (will be encrypted with the freeradius shared secret,

          can be tunneled via TLS for increased security if needed). FreeRADIUS
          in

          turn will send the username and password via HTTPS to this endpoint.
        heading: api_v1_freeradius_authorize_create
      - content: |-
          **API Endpoint used by FreeRADIUS server.**
          Returns an empty response body in order to instruct
          FreeRADIUS to avoid processing the response body.
        heading: api_v1_freeradius_postauth_create

          Credentials):

          {
              "hostname": "nexapp-fw-01",
              "primary_ip": "192.168.1.1",
              "secondary_ip": "192.168.1.2",
              "password": "...",
              "ha_interface": "lan",

      - content: >-
          The stock IPAM API only exposes addresses nested under a single subnet

          (`ipam/subnet/<id>/ip-address/`), so a fleet-wide list was impossible.
          This

          is read-only on purpose: creating an address still needs the subnet in
          the

          URL, so writes keep using the nested route.


          Filters mirror the admin's: `?subnet=`, `?organization=`, `?search=`.
        heading: every-ip-address-across-subnets--what-the-admin-changelist-shows
      - content: >-
          `?master_subnet=<uuid>` is what the subnet form uses — a rule only
          makes

          sense in the context of the subnet it divides, so the form never wants
          the

          unfiltered list.
        heading: list-rules-optionally-narrowed-to-one-master-subnet
      - content: >-
          `?master_subnet=<uuid>` is what the subnet form uses — a rule only
          makes

          sense in the context of the subnet it divides, so the form never wants
          the

          unfiltered list.
        heading: list-rules-optionally-narrowed-to-one-master-subnet-1
      - content: |-
          Read-only and additive: writes still go to the stock `ipam/subnet/`
          endpoints. This exists only because the changelist needs a column the
          default serializer does not expose.
        heading: subnets-with-their-related-device-resolved
      - content: The configurations a CHART can use — the admin's chart inline dropdown.
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_chart-configuration_list
      - content: >-
          Read-only and not device-scoped: the list is a settings constant,
          identical

          for every device. Authentication still applies (ProtectedAPIMixin),
          but no

          model permission — knowing the names of the check classes discloses
          nothing

          a logged-in user cannot already see on the admin form.
        heading: the-check-types-a-check-can-be-set-to--the-admin-dropdowns-options
      - content: >-
          Mirrors the admin's Content type field, which offers every content
          type in

          the install. Only Device (and its proxies) actually validate —
          everything

          else is rejected by BaseCheck.validate_instance() on save — but the
          choice

          is exposed as the admin exposes it rather than pre-filtered here.


          Read-only and not device-scoped, like CheckTypeListView above.
        heading: content-types-for-the-checks-generic-relation--the-admin-dropdown
      - content: |-
          Normalize timezone before delegating to MonitoringApiViewMixin.get(),
          so InfluxDB never sees invalid timezones like Asia/Calcutta.
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_dashboard_list
      - content: |-
          Supports session authentication and token authentication.

          `NOTE:` The response does not include the information and health
          status of the specific metrics, this information can be retrieved in
          the detail endpoint of each device.
        heading: lists-devices-and-their-monitoring-status-health-status
      - content: >-
          No DELETE: an AlertSettings row belongs to its metric (OneToOne,
          created

          with it). Removing alerting is what ``is_active=False`` is for.
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_device_alert-settings_read
      - content: >-
          No DELETE: an AlertSettings row belongs to its metric (OneToOne,
          created

          with it). Removing alerting is what ``is_active=False`` is for.
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_device_alert-settings_partial_update
      - content: >-
          No DELETE: an AlertSettings row belongs to its metric (OneToOne,
          created

          with it). Removing alerting is what ``is_active=False`` is for.
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_device_alert-settings_update
      - content: >-
          Query string: either ``range`` (one of RANGES, default 7d) or an
          explicit

          ``start`` and ``end``.


          Returns ``get_device_availability``'s report unchanged — window,
          events,

          timeline, and the ``friendly`` block (intervals + summary) the Events
          table

          is built from. Passing it through rather than reshaping keeps one
          definition

          of "uptime" between the admin page and the new UI.
        heading: get-apiv1monitoringdeviceidavailability
      - content: 'Underlay performance: WAN uptime timeline, path switch history, SLA,
          live health.'
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_device_underlay-performance_list

          Retrieve device information, monitoring status (health status), a list

          ``?status=true``).

          device key authentication passed as query string parameter (this
          method is meant to be used by network devices).

      - content: >-
          Mirrors GlobalTopAppsView — same window parsing, same visibility
          rules,

          same envelope — because a category is the coarser grouping of the very

          rows that view reports, and the two must agree.
        heading: fleet-traffic-grouped-by-dpi-category
      - content: |-
          IPSec tunnel status for all devices in the user's organizations.
          Manager-only access.
        heading: api_v1_monitoring_ipsec-tunnels-status_list
      - content: >-
          A registered key ("ping", "disk") rather than free text, so the client
          must

          choose from this list. Served rather than hardcoded because the set
          comes

          from the monitoring app's registry and a deployment can extend it.
        heading: the-configurations-a-metric-can-use--the-admins-dropdown
      - content: >-
          Backs the admin's "Recover deleted metrics" screen. django-reversion
          keeps

          a Version row for every tracked save; a metric whose id no longer
          exists in

          the table but still has versions is a deletion that can be undone.


          Only the most recent version per object is listed — that is the state
          a

          recovery would restore.
        heading: metrics-that-were-deleted-but-are-still-recoverable
      - content: >-
          ``Version.revert()`` writes the stored fields back under the original

          primary key, so anything that referenced the metric by id lines up
          again.

          Charts and alert settings are separate objects with their own versions
          and

          are NOT brought back by this — the admin behaves the same way.
        heading: restore-one-deleted-metric-from-its-last-version
      - content: >-
          POST mirrors the admin's "ADD METRIC". A hand-made metric has no

          time-series behind it until the pipeline writes one, which is equally
          true

          of the admin's form.
        heading: every-metric-newest-first--the-admin-changelists-default-ordering
      - content: >-
          POST mirrors the admin's "ADD METRIC". A hand-made metric has no

          time-series behind it until the pipeline writes one, which is equally
          true

          of the admin's form.
        heading: every-metric-newest-first--the-admin-changelists-default-ordering-1
      - content: >-
          Update mirrors the admin's change form, which edits the same four
          fields

          the add form exposes plus the advanced pair. Health flags and tags
          stay

          read-only — those are pipeline state.


          Delete is offered because the admin offers it — but note the
          asymmetry:

          the admin wraps deletion in django-reversion ("Recover deleted
          metrics"),

          and this endpoint has no such safety net. A deleted metric is gone
          along

          with the alert settings and charts that cascade from it.
        heading: read-update-or-delete-one-metric

      - content: >-
          No update — every field is written by the monitoring pipeline as
          clients

          associate and disconnect, which is why the admin's change page renders

          them all read-only and offers only Delete.
        heading: one-session-read-or-delete

          clients

        heading: api_v1_notifications_notification_dnd_update
      - content: Return all DND entries for user (global + per-org).

      - content: Show config diff between this deploy and the previous deploy for the
          same device.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_deployment_diff
      - content: |-
          Uses ns.commit.commit-config (hook-free per-package commit) so this
          is fast and only touches the packages the user actually modified.
        heading: commit-the-tracked-pending-uci-packages-on-the-router-via-sshubus
      - content: List / Add / Delete / Toggle GRE tunnels on the device via SSH + UCI.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_gre_tunnels_read
      - content: List / Add / Delete / Toggle GRE tunnels on the device via SSH + UCI.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_gre_tunnels_create
      - content: >-
          Router's port-8181 bridge does NOT whitelist /api-new/l2tp_server

          (returns 404), so we use UCI directly: `uci show l2tp_server` for

          GET, `uci set` + commit for POST.


          Schema (single section):
              config config 'config'
                  option service 'enable'|'disable'
                  option ip_start <ipv4>
                  option ip_end <ipv4>
                  option local_ip <ipv4>
                  option mtu <int>
                  option mru <int>
                  option username <str>
                  option password <str>
                  option lcp_interval <int>
                  option lcp_failure <int>
                  option require_mschap_v2 '0'|'1'
                  option require_chap '0'|'1'
                  option require_pap '0'|'1'
                  option defailtroute '0'|'1'   # router uses this exact (mis)spelling
                  option ipdefault '0'|'1'
                  option proxyarp '0'|'1'
        heading: l2tp-server-tunnel--read--write-via-ssh--etcconfigl2tp_server
      - content: >-
          Router's port-8181 bridge does NOT whitelist /api-new/l2tp_server

          (returns 404), so we use UCI directly: `uci show l2tp_server` for

          GET, `uci set` + commit for POST.


          Schema (single section):
              config config 'config'
                  option service 'enable'|'disable'
                  option ip_start <ipv4>
                  option ip_end <ipv4>
                  option local_ip <ipv4>
                  option mtu <int>
                  option mru <int>
                  option username <str>
                  option password <str>
                  option lcp_interval <int>
                  option lcp_failure <int>
                  option require_mschap_v2 '0'|'1'
                  option require_chap '0'|'1'
                  option require_pap '0'|'1'
                  option defailtroute '0'|'1'   # router uses this exact (mis)spelling
                  option ipdefault '0'|'1'
                  option proxyarp '0'|'1'
        heading: l2tp-server-tunnel--read--write-via-ssh--etcconfigl2tp_server-1
      - content: >-
          Router's port-8181 bridge does NOT whitelist /api-new/l2tp_server

          (returns 404), so we use UCI directly: `uci show l2tp_server` for

          Schema (single section):
              config config 'config'

                  option ipdefault '0'|'1'
                  option proxyarp '0'|'1'
        heading: l2tp-server-tunnel--read--write-via-ssh--etcconfigl2tp_server-2

          List / Add / Edit / Delete / Toggle L2TP CLIENT tunnels via the
          router's `/api-new/l2tp` GoAhead endpoint (no SSH, no UCI parsing).
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_l2tp_tunnels_read
      - content: |-
          List / Add / Edit / Delete / Toggle L2TP CLIENT tunnels via the
          router's `/api-new/l2tp` GoAhead endpoint (no SSH, no UCI parsing).
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_l2tp_tunnels_create
      - content: Return the list of UCI packages this device has dirty in staging.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_pending_changes
      - content: |-
          Discards staging back to /etc/config/ contents. Same packages-list
          contract as apply-pending/.
        heading: revert-the-tracked-pending-uci-packages-on-the-router-via-sshubus
      - content: |-
          Combined GET/POST for SLA Settings — DRF routes both verbs to one
          action; we dispatch internally so GET and POST share the same URL.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_sla-settings_read
      - content: |-
          Combined GET/POST for SLA Settings — DRF routes both verbs to one
          action; we dispatch internally so GET and POST share the same URL.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_device_sla-settings_create
      - content: |-
          Reads & writes `/etc/config/vxlan` — the same UCI package the
          router's standalone VPN→VXLAN web UI manages (handled by
          /usr/sbin/vxlan_mg). Live link state is cross-referenced via the
          `network.interface` ubus dump (whitelisted on the port-8181 bridge
          — no extra SSH for reads).

          POST body: {"action": "add"|"delete", ...payload}
        heading: list--add--delete-vxlan-tunnels-on-the-device
      - content: |-
          Reads & writes `/etc/config/vxlan` — the same UCI package the
          router's standalone VPN→VXLAN web UI manages (handled by
          /usr/sbin/vxlan_mg). Live link state is cross-referenced via the
          `network.interface` ubus dump (whitelisted on the port-8181 bridge
          — no extra SSH for reads).

          POST body: {"action": "add"|"delete", ...payload}
        heading: list--add--delete-vxlan-tunnels-on-the-device-1
      - content: |-
          GET  -> {enabled, networks: [...]}
          POST -> {"action": "join"|"leave"|"enable"|"disable", "nwid": "..."}

          Writes go through zerotier-cli AND uci, because the two hold different
        heading: oobm--zerotier-overlay-state-read-over-ssh
      - content: |-
          GET  -> {enabled, networks: [...]}
          POST -> {"action": "join"|"leave"|"enable"|"disable", "nwid": "..."}

          Writes go through zerotier-cli AND uci, because the two hold different
        heading: oobm--zerotier-overlay-state-read-over-ssh-1
      - content: |-
          Org-scoped through the parent device's organization. The ``upsert``
          action lets the WAN Uplinks page assign / unassign a label by
          ``(hostname, interface_name)`` without first looking up the row id.

          GET    /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/
          POST   /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/
          GET    /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/<id>/
          PATCH  /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/<id>/
          DELETE /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/<id>/
          POST   /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/upsert/
        heading: crud-for-per-device-interface_name-path-label-assignments
      - content: |-
          Org-scoped through the parent device's organization. The ``upsert``
          action lets the WAN Uplinks page assign / unassign a label by
          ``(hostname, interface_name)`` without first looking up the row id.

          GET    /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/
          POST   /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/
          GET    /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/<id>/
          PATCH  /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/<id>/

          action lets the WAN Uplinks page assign / unassign a label by
          ``(hostname, interface_name)`` without first looking up the row id.

          GET    /api/v1/nsbond/interface-path-label/

          names.

      - content: >-
          The device has no edit action for these — a change there is delete+add
          —

          but the controller stores them as ordinary rows, so PATCH is allowed
          here

          and the push layer is what must translate an update into delete+add.

        heading: api_v1_nsbond_sdwan-fabric_list
      - content: >-
          GET /api/v1/nsbond/sdwan-topologies/ — list topologies (label,

          organization, format, strategy, published, node/link counts,
          timestamps).
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_sdwan-topologies_list
      - content: |-
          GET /api/v1/nsbond/sdwan-topology/<id>/ — the topology's NetJSON graph
          (nodes + links with status/properties), as rendered by the visualizer.
        heading: api_v1_nsbond_sdwan-topology_read
      - content: >-
          Not org-scoped by the Dual mixin: this model has no topology or device
          to

          reach an organization through — it carries `organization` directly,
          which

          the filterset exposes. Superusers see everything; the queryset below
          is

          narrowed to the caller's organizations plus the fleet-wide rows.
        heading: fleet-level-sla-settings-authored-in-the-policy-engine

      - content: >-
          Delete is offered because the admin has it, but note what the model
          does:

          RadiusBatch.delete() calls self.users.all().delete(), so removing a
          batch

          removes every user it generated. The UI says so before asking.
        heading: list-and-delete-user-batches-creation-lives-on-radiusbatch

          Delete is offered because the admin has it, but note what the model
          does:

          RadiusBatch.delete() calls self.users.all().delete(), so removing a
          batch

        heading: list-and-delete-user-batches-creation-lives-on-radiusbatch-1

      - content: 'Read-only: FreeRADIUS writes this log, nothing else should.'
        heading: api_v1_radius-admin_post-auth_list
      - content: 'Read-only: FreeRADIUS writes this log, nothing else should.'
        heading: api_v1_radius-admin_post-auth_read
      - content: |-
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Allows organization administrators to create
          a batch of users using a csv file or generate users
          with a given prefix.
        heading: api_v1_radius_batch_create
      - content: Used by users to create new accounts, usually to access the internet.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_create
      - content: |-
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Allows users to change their password after using
          the `Reset password` endpoint.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_password_change_create
      - content: |-
          This is the classic "password forgotten recovery feature" which
          sends a reset password token to the email of the user.
          The input field can be an email, an username or
          a phone number (if mobile phone verification is in use).
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_password_reset_create
      - content: |-
          Allows users to confirm their reset password after having
          it requested via the `Reset password` endpoint.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_password_reset_confirm_create
      - content: |
          
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Allows users to change their phone number, will flag the
          user as inactive and send them a verification code via SMS.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_phone_change_create
      - content: |
          
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Used for SMS verification, sends a code via SMS to the
          phone number of the user.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_phone_token_create
      - content: |
          
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Used for SMS verification, allows checking whether an active
          SMS token was already requested for the mobile phone number
          of the logged in account.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_phone_token_active_list
      - content: |-
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Used for SMS verification, allows users to validate the
          code they receive via SMS.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_phone_verify_create
      - content: |
          
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Returns the radius sessions of the logged-in user and the organization
          specified in the URL.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_session_list
      - content: Obtain the user radius token required for authentication in APIs.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_token_create
      - content: Used to check whether the auth token of a user is valid or not.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_token_validate_create
      - content: |
          
          **Requires the user auth token (Bearer Token).**
          Returns the user's RADIUS usage and limit for the organization.
          It executes the relevant counters and returns returns information that
          shows how much time and/or traffic the user has consumed.
        heading: api_v1_radius_organization_account_usage_read
      - content: |
          
          Returns all RADIUS sessions of user managed organizations.
        heading: api_v1_radius_sessions_list
      - content: Aggregated security stats across all features (last 30 days).
        heading: api_v1_security_alert_dashboard
      - content: Deploy Instashield config to device via ns.threatshield RPCD.
        heading: api_v1_security_threatshield_deploy
      - content: CRUD plus bulk CSV import for the admission allowlist.
        heading: api_v1_serial-admission_allowed-serial_list
      - content: CRUD plus bulk CSV import for the admission allowlist.
        heading: api_v1_serial-admission_allowed-serial_create
      - content: The blank template an operator edits and uploads back.
        heading: api_v1_serial-admission_allowed-serial_csv_template
      - content: |-
          Answers 200 with per-row errors rather than 400 on a partial file: a
          50-row CSV with two bad rows imports the other 48, and the caller is
          told exactly which two failed and why.
        heading: bulk-import-the-rest-twin-of-the-admins-upload-csv-view

      - content: Returns avg latency/jitter/loss for last X days.
        heading: api_v1_spoke_stats_list
      - content: >-
          Any authenticated user may call it; what they get back is already
          narrowed

          to their own organizations by ``_visible_orgs``. Superusers see every
          org,

          which is what ``is_superuser`` in the payload lets the UI label.
        heading: get-apiv1systemlicense-summary--entitlements--release-catalogue

      - content: |-
          Synchronous fan-out via the celery task — returns a per-device
          summary in the same response so the operator sees results
          immediately. Use ?device_ids=<uuid>,<uuid> to scope to specific
          devices; omit to target every device with a management_ip.
        heading: direct-push-of-the-current-config-to-every-reachable-router
      - content: |-
          Reads /usr/bin/chronyc -n sources -v on the controller host.
          Returns a list of dicts: state, name, stratum, reach, last_rx,
          last_sample. State is single-char per chrony: * + - ? x ~.
        heading: live-chronyc-sources-parsed-into-json
      - content: |-
          Reads /usr/bin/chronyc -c tracking (CSV) on the controller host.
          Used by the admin Live Status tab; safe to poll every 30 s.
        heading: live-chronyc-tracking-parsed-into-json
      - content: >-
          Read and write only; no create/delete. Django keeps exactly one Site
          row

          referenced by SITE_ID, and adding or removing rows breaks every

          {site.domain} placeholder in the alert templates rather than doing

          anything useful.


          Superuser-only for the same reason NtpServerConfig is: one row, global

          blast radius across every organization's outgoing email.
        heading: the-controllers-own-identity-domain--display-name
      - content: |-
          Saves the client from guessing which of the rows is live when a
          deployment has more than one.
        heading: the-row-site_id-points-at--what-the-ui-actually-edits
      - content: >-
          Read and write only; no create/delete. Django keeps exactly one Site
          row

      - content: >-
          Its own endpoint rather than a writable nested list on the config:
          editing

          one slot through the parent would mean sending all three back, and any

          client that forgot one would silently delete it.
        heading: the-primarysecondarytertiary-slots-of-a-client-config
